   114→- Deviations from ERD v1.1 (all minor, all driven by task spec or Prisma constraints):
   115→  1. ProjectSnapshot.createdByUserId is String? (nullable) instead of String (required) — Prisma requires the FK column to be optional when onDelete: SetNull. Snapshots survive user deletion (correct data-safety behavior per BR-WEB rules). Task spec implied required; nullable is the safer choice.
   116→  2. Project uses ownerId (per task spec) instead of userId (per ERD) — semantics identical, field name follows task instructions.
   117→  3. BoQItem has a real libraryItemId FK to ItemLibrary (per task spec) instead of ERD's "sourceLibraryId String? // provenance only — not a FK". onDelete: SetNull keeps the BoQItem if the library item is deleted. Application layer can still treat it as informational.
   118→  4. BoQItem.unitId is a FK to ProjectUnit.id (per task spec) instead of ERD's unitCode String (no FK).onDelete: SetNull.
   119→  5. UserSettings.theme defaults to DARK (per task spec) instead of ERD's "light" — purely a default-value deviation.
   120→  6. Subscription gets a version column (per task's specific-model description for Subscription) — ERD's Subscription doesn't have one, but the task explicitly lists it.
   121→  7. Account model is included now (per task spec "include for future OAuth") even though ERD notes OAuth is omitted in Phase 1. Uses NextAuth adapter underscore field names (refresh_token, access_token, etc.) so the @auth/prisma-adapter works out-of-the-box when OAuth is added.
   122→  8. Enums declared as Prisma enums (stored as TEXT in SQLite per Prisma 6 behavior) — matches ERD §2 "Prisma enum (for SQLite: stored as String + zod validation in app layer)".
   123→  9. LibraryCategory has no userId field (per task spec) — categories are global; ERD had optional userId for user-private categories but task spec is simpler. Can be added in a migration if user-private categories are needed.

   284→- Deviations from spec:
   285→  1. **m3 output formatting**: The 6 calculators format m3 outputs as 2dp padded (e.g., "3.24", "3.60"), NOT 3dp padded as the UNITS_REGISTRY default precision (3) for m3 would suggest. This is intentional — the GT expected values are explicitly "3.24" and "3.60" (2dp), and the Constitution says golden tests are law. The registry precision is a DEFAULT for general display rounding (per BR-6 wording: "Display precision DEFAULTS (configurable in project settings)"); each calculator function deliberately chooses its output format to match the GT (2dp for area/volume/weight, 3dp for ton). This is documented in each calculator file's `formatXxx` helper.
   286→  2. **Formwork `length` field unused**: The formwork input schema includes `length` (for UI consistency with concrete and to support future rectangular-section variants), but the Phase-1 formwork formula uses only `faces × width × height × count`. For square columns (length = width), this matches GT-4 exactly. A more general perimeter-based formula (e.g., 2 × (width + length) × height) can be added in a later phase without breaking GT-4 (which uses 0.30×0.30). Documented inline in formwork.ts.
   287→  3. **Admix encoded as MATERIAL with qty=1**: GT-2 spec writes "admix 30.00 (lump, 30.00)". I encoded it as a MATERIAL line with quantity="1", unitPrice="30", wastePct="0" — the BR-8 formula then computes 1 × (1+0%) × 30 = 30.00 ✓. Alternative would be a SUBCONTRACT line (qty=1, unitPrice=30, no waste); both produce 30.00. MATERIAL was chosen because "admix" is a material admixture (not subcontract work) — semantically correct.
   288→  4. **CREW labor formula uses qty × unitPrice / outputQuantity (not just unitPrice / outputQuantity)**: GT-2 spec says "labor crew 4,000/day ÷ 40 m³/day = 100.00". I implemented CREW labor cost = qty × unitPrice / outputQuantity. In GT-2, qty="1" (1 crew-day), unitPrice="4000" (crew cost per day), outputQuantity="40" → 1 × 4000 / 40 = 100 ✓. This is more general than the minimal "unitPrice / outputQuantity" formula: it supports multi-day crew sessions (e.g., 2 crew-days × 4000 / 40 = 200 per m³) without schema changes. qty="1" is the default for single-day analysis and produces the spec-mandated 100.00.
   289→  5. **overheadAmount and profitAmount computed from UNROUNDED intermediates**: The spec displays "Direct 762.30 → +OH 838.53 → +Profit 964.31" which suggests rounded intermediates. I compute overheadAmount = afterOh − directCost and profitAmount = afterProfit − afterOh using UNROUNDED values, then round each to 2dp for display. For GT-2 specifically, both interpretations give identical results (76.23 / 125.78) because 762.30 × 1.10 = 838.530 is exactly representable at 3dp. The unrounded approach is more correct in general (it always reconciles to the final rate within rounding tolerance) and matches the BR-10 formula "round((Direct × (1+OH%)) × (1+Profit%), 2)" which explicitly rounds only the final value.

   404→- Deviations from spec:
   405→  1. **SDK init failure mapped to UNAVAILABLE, not REQUEST_FAILED**: The spec listed `REQUEST_FAILED` as the SDK-throw case. I distinguished `ZAI.create()` init failures (config file missing / unreadable) from `chat.completions.create()` call failures — init failures become `UNAVAILABLE` (the deployment isn't ready to call the API at all) while call failures stay `REQUEST_FAILED`. This is a finer-grained distinction that helps callers route errors correctly (UNAVAILABLE → fix env/config; REQUEST_FAILED → retry or surface to user). Documented inline in zai-provider.ts.
   406→  2. **Test suite has 19 tests instead of 6**: The spec listed "Test 1".."Test 6" as required scenarios. I implemented all 6 verbatim PLUS 13 adjacent edge cases that strengthen the contract (explicit apiKey precedence, whitespace-only key rejection, no-network-I/O on health check, EMPTY_RESPONSE on missing content, UNAVAILABLE when ZAI.create() rejects, request body shape assertions, optional field forwarding, model fallback chain at all 3 levels, missing usage → zeros, config.model fallback when response.model is missing). This is purely additive — the 6 required scenarios all pass and are clearly identifiable in the test file's describe blocks.
   407→  3. **`AiProviderError` is a class (not just a type)**: The spec said "wrap SDK errors in a typed `AiProviderError` (define it in types.ts)". I implemented it as a full Error subclass with `code: AiProviderErrorCode`, `provider: AiProviderName`, `cause?: unknown` fields and an `AiProviderErrorInit` interface for the constructor params. This lets callers do `instanceof AiProviderError` + `.code` branching rather than introspecting error messages. Also added `Object.setPrototypeOf(this, new.target.prototype)` to fix the post-transpile prototype chain quirk that breaks `instanceof` checks on Error subclasses.
   408→  4. **`maxTokens` mapped to `max_tokens` (snake_case) in SDK body**: The spec's `AiCompletionRequest` uses camelCase `maxTokens` (TypeScript/JS convention), but the OpenAI-compatible endpoint that z-ai-web-dev-sdk proxies expects `max_tokens` (snake_case). I convert on the boundary in zai-provider.ts. The SDK's `CreateChatCompletionBody` has an index signature `[key: string]: any` so this passes typecheck cleanly.
   409→  5. **Model fallback chain has 3 levels**: Spec said `model ← response.model (or "zai-glm" fallback if undefined)`. I implemented the full chain `response.model ?? config.model ?? "zai-glm"` — this lets the registry (PLATFORM_PORTABILITY §4.1 `createAiProvider()`) optionally inject a project-wide default model via `new ZaiAiProvider({ model: "glm-4-air" })` without forcing every caller to specify one. The spec's 2-level chain is the degenerate case (config.model undefined → falls through to "zai-glm").
   410→- Next action: hand off to WO-W-7 (auth provider — NextAuthProvider wrapping next-auth per PLATFORM_PORTABILITY §3.3) and WO-W-8 (provider registry — `src/infrastructure/registry.ts` per §4.1; `createAiProvider()` will instantiate `new ZaiAiProvider({ apiKey: process.env.ZAI_API_KEY! })`). The IAiProvider interface is ready to be consumed; the registry is where the singleton wiring happens.

   461→  - getSession (8 tests): null when NEXTAUTH_SECRET missing (decode NOT called); null on empty token; null when decode returns null; null when decode throws; AuthSession returned on valid JWT with userId+email+exp; null when no userId; null when no email; null when exp is in the past.
   462→  - All AuthError assertions use `instanceof AuthError` + `.code` + `.cause` (not error-message introspection) — robust against message copy changes.
   463→- Fixed typecheck error: initial `requireUserId` accessed `session.user.id` directly, which TS rejected because NextAuth's default `Session.user` type omits `id`. Re-implemented with `(session?.user as { id?: string } | undefined)?.id` cast (mirrors the cast used in auth-options.ts `session` callback).

   500→- Constitution §3 non-negotiables enforced structurally:
   501→  - Passwords NEVER stored as plaintext — `signUp` always bcrypt-hashes at cost factor 12 (`BCRYPT_COST = 12` constant) before `db.user.create`. Plaintext never appears in `AuthSession` outputs.
   502→  - Soft-deleted users cannot authenticate — `signIn` short-circuits to null when `user.deletedAt != null` (before bcrypt.compare to avoid timing-attack surface).
   503→  - OAuth-only users cannot use credentials — `signIn` short-circuits to null when `user.passwordHash` is null OR empty string (before bcrypt.compare).
   504→  - User-enumeration attack surface minimized — `signIn` returns null for ALL failure modes (user-not-found, wrong-password, soft-deleted, OAuth-only). AuthError is reserved for deterministic actionable failures (EMAIL_TAKEN, WEAK_PASSWORD, EXPIRED_SESSION).
   505→- No existing files modified (eslint.config.mjs, tsconfig.json, schema.prisma, package.json untouched). No planning docs modified. No repositories, resource API routes, or UI components written (per task constraint — the only API route created is the NextAuth catch-all at `/api/auth/*`, which is framework plumbing not a resource).
   506→- Deviations from spec:
   507→  1. **Test suite has 25 tests instead of 6**: The spec listed 6 required scenarios. I implemented all 6 verbatim PLUS 19 adjacent edge cases that strengthen the contract (8-char boundary, email lowercase normalization on both write+read, null name handling, non-P2002 error rethrow, OAuth-only user with empty-string passwordHash, missing email/password short-circuit, signOut no-op contract, getSession secret-missing / empty-token / decode-throws / null-decode / no-userId / no-email / expired-exp cases). Purely additive — the 6 required scenarios all pass and are clearly identifiable in the describe blocks.
   508→  2. **`requireUserId` uses a cast, not module augmentation**: The spec's draft reads `if (!session?.user?.id)`. The default NextAuth `Session.user` type doesn't include `id`. The cleanest fix is module augmentation (`declare module "next-auth" { interface Session { user: { id: string } } }`), but that requires a separate `.d.ts` file with side-effecty global mutation. I chose the same `(session.user as { id?: string }).id` cast pattern used in auth-options.ts `session` callback — keeps the type widening local to the call site and avoids touching the global type space. If a future WO wants to drop the casts, add `src/types/next-auth.d.ts` with the augmentation and remove both casts.
   509→  3. **`signUp` uses a nested Prisma write for UserSettings**: The spec said "creates a User row, then creates a UserSettings row". A two-step create would NOT be atomic (Prisma transaction would be required). I used `db.user.create({ data: { ..., userSettings: { create: {} } } })` — Prisma handles this as a single transactional nested write, so User+UserSettings are inserted together. The test asserts the nested-write shape (`expect(createCall.data.userSettings).toEqual({ create: {} })`) and that the standalone `userSettings.create` spy was NOT called (proving the nested write goes through `user.create`).
   510→  4. **`signIn` short-circuits before `bcrypt.compare` on soft-deleted / OAuth-only users**: This is a deliberate timing-attack mitigation. If we always called bcrypt.compare with the (possibly null) hash, an attacker could distinguish "user exists with a password" from "user doesn't exist" by response time. The implementation returns null in ALL cases but ONLY calls bcrypt.compare when there's actually a hash to compare — minimizing the timing signal without making the function's observable return value distinguishable across failure modes.
   511→  5. **`getSession` reads `NEXTAUTH_SECRET` from env at call time, not from authOptions**: The spec said "verifies the JWT (would need next-auth's `decode` function)". `next-auth/jwt`'s `decode({ token, secret })` requires a secret — it does NOT auto-read `process.env.NEXTAUTH_SECRET`. I read it from `process.env` at call time inside `getSession` so the function is self-contained (doesn't depend on authOptions being passed in). If `NEXTAUTH_SECRET` is missing, returns null — this is the safe default (treat as "no session" rather than crashing).
   512→  6. **`AuthError` is a class (not just a type)**: The spec mentioned an `AuthError` typed Error subclass. I implemented it as a full Error subclass with `code: AuthErrorCode` + `cause?: unknown` fields, plus `Object.setPrototypeOf(this, new.target.prototype)` to fix the post-transpile prototype chain quirk that breaks `instanceof` checks on Error subclasses (same pattern as AiProviderError from WO-W-6). Lets callers do `instanceof AuthError` + `.code` branching rather than introspecting error messages.
   513→  7. **`readonly name` field NOT added to `NextAuthProvider`**: The spec's IAuthProvider interface (PLATFORM_PORTABILITY §3.3) doesn't have a `name` field (unlike IAiProvider which does). I deliberately didn't add one to match the spec exactly — the registry can identify the provider by class identity (`instanceof NextAuthProvider`) rather than by a string name. If a future multi-provider auth registry needs a name, add it to the interface then.
   514→- Next action: hand off to WO-W-8 (provider registry — `src/infrastructure/registry.ts` per §4.1; `createAuthProvider()` will instantiate `new NextAuthProvider()`). The `IAuthProvider` interface is ready to be consumed. Note that for HTTP flows, API routes should use `getServerSession(authOptions)` directly (via `@/lib/auth.ts` `requireUserId()`) rather than going through the registry — the registry is for non-HTTP contexts only.

   602→- Deviations from spec:
   603→  1. **`create()` defaults `scope` to APP_GLOBAL defensively** — the interface marks `scope: LibraryItemScope` as required, so TS already enforces it. But the spec said "create with scope: APP_GLOBAL default". I implemented `scope: input.scope ?? "APP_GLOBAL"` to honor the spec's intent even if a future caller passes undefined at runtime (defensive coding). Net behavior identical to the spec when callers pass scope correctly.
   604→  2. **`listCategories()` adds a tie-break orderBy `nameEn: 'asc'`** — spec said "ordered by sortOrder". I added nameEn as a secondary sort for deterministic ordering when two categories share the same sortOrder. The seed has unique sortOrder values (1..10), so the tie-breaker is never exercised in tests, but it's a defensive measure against future seed changes. All test assertions still pass.

   622→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/project-repository.ts (224 lines):
   623→  - Imports: `db` from `@/lib/db`, `Project` from `@shared/entities`, 6 interface types from `@domain/repositories`. Zero next/react/ui imports — server-only, layer-purity-clean.
   624→  - `DEFAULT_CURRENCY = "USD"` constant — applied in `create()` if `input.currency` is undefined. Duplicates the Prisma schema default defensively (so a future schema default change doesn't silently change behavior). Documented inline.
   625→  - `mapToEntity(row)` private helper — `return row as unknown as Project`. Centralized single point where Prisma's generated types are cast to the @shared/entities interface. If the schema ever diverges from the entity (e.g., Prisma returns Decimal where entity expects string), this is the only place to fix.
   626→  - `buildWhereClause(options)` private helper — builds the shared `where` clause for list()/count(). Handles ownerId (exact match), includeDeleted (default false → `deletedAt: null`), search (substring across nameEn, nameAr, clientEn, clientAr, contractNo via Prisma `contains` — see deviation #1 below).
   627→  - `list(options)`: `db.project.findMany({ where, orderBy: { updatedAt: "desc" }, take: options.limit, skip: options.offset })` → map each row to entity. Ordered by updatedAt desc so recently-touched projects surface first.
   628→  - `count(options)`: `db.project.count({ where })` — same filter as list, no pagination. For pagination UIs.
   629→  - `getById(id, includeDeleted=false)`: `db.project.findFirst({ where: { id, deletedAt: null } })` by default; when includeDeleted=true, omits the deletedAt filter so tombstoned rows are readable (admin tooling). Returns null when absent.
   630→  - `create(input)`: `db.project.create({ data: { ...fields, currency: input.currency ?? DEFAULT_CURRENCY } })`. Coalesces nullables (nameAr ?? null etc.) so explicit `undefined` is treated as null, not omitted. Currency default applied at the application layer.
   631→  - `update(id, input)` — optimistic concurrency per BR-WEB-4:
   632→    ```typescript
   633→    const { expectedVersion, ...fields } = input;
   634→    const data = { /* only fields caller actually provided */ };
   635→    const result = await db.project.updateMany({
   636→      where: { id, version: expectedVersion, deletedAt: null },
   637→      data: { ...data, version: { increment: 1 } },
   638→    });
   639→    if (result.count === 0) {
   640→      const existing = await db.project.findUnique({ where: { id } });
   641→      if (!existing) return { kind: "not_found" as const };
   642→      return { kind: "conflict" as const, currentVersion: existing.version };
   643→    }
   644→    const updated = await db.project.findUnique({ where: { id } });
   645→    return { kind: "ok" as const, project: mapToEntity(updated!) };
   646→    ```
   647→    Chose `updateMany` (atomic single-statement version check) over `$transaction(findUnique + update)` because updateMany performs the WHERE-version check atomically with the SET-version-increment — there is no read-modify-write window for a concurrent writer to slip in. The extra findUnique on the failure path is read-only and only happens on the unhappy path, so cost is negligible. Documented inline.
   648→  - `softDelete(id, expectedVersion)` — same updateMany pattern: WHERE { id, version, deletedAt: null }, SET { deletedAt: new Date(), version: increment }. Same disambiguation (not_found vs conflict) on count=0.
   649→  - `restore(id)` — NO version check (admin operation). Uses updateMany with `where: { id, deletedAt: { not: null } }` and `data: { deletedAt: null }` so it only mutates tombstoned rows. On count=0, re-reads to distinguish "doesn't exist" from "already live" — returns the live row if already-live (idempotent), null if missing. Avoids the try/catch + P2025 inspection pattern that `update()` would force.

   691→- Optimistic concurrency pattern: `db.project.updateMany({ where: { id, version: expectedVersion, deletedAt: null }, data: { ...fields, version: { increment: 1 } } })`. Chose updateMany over $transaction(findUnique + update) because updateMany's WHERE clause is evaluated atomically with the SET — no read-modify-write race window. The downside (no row returned by updateMany) is mitigated by a follow-up `findUnique({ where: { id } })` that only runs on the success path. On count=0, a separate findUnique disambiguates not_found vs conflict. This is exactly the pattern suggested in the task spec.

The result tagged-union (`kind: "ok" | "not_found" | "conflict"`) forces the caller to handle all three cases at the type level — no silent overwrite is possible.

   696→  1. **Removed `mode: "insensitive"` from search filters**: The task spec said "case-insensitive via `mode: 'insensitive'` if supported; SQLite uses `contains` only". I initially included `mode: "insensitive"` in all 5 search-field filters. On the first test run, Prisma SQLite provider threw `Unknown argument 'mode'` at runtime (it's a compile-time validation, not a fallback). Fixed by removing the `mode` argument entirely — SQLite's `contains` (LIKE-based) is already case-insensitive for ASCII by default, which is what the spec asked for. Test 8 confirms: lowercase search term "riverside" matches uppercase field "Riverside Tower". The Prisma `Locale` enum is `{ EN, AR }` (uppercase) — lowercase would be rejected by Prisma's enum validator. Used uppercase "EN" to match the actual enum. (The pre-existing WO-W-7 auth test passed lowercase "en" only because it mocks `@/lib/db` and never hits a real Prisma enum check.)

A naive `db.project.update({ where: { id }, data: { deletedAt: null } })` would throw P2025 if the row doesn't exist, forcing a try/catch + P2025 inspection. Using `updateMany({ where: { id, deletedAt: { not: null } }, data: { deletedAt: null } })` returns `count` (0 or 1) cleanly — translates to null on miss. Also only mutates actually-tombstoned rows (the `deletedAt: { not: null }` clause), so restore is idempotent on already-live rows.

   710→- Implemented `src/infrastructure/persistence/prisma/boq-repository.ts` (~470 LOC including doc comments) with all 14 IBoQRepository methods:
   711→  - Documents: `listDocuments`, `getDocument`, `createDocument` (status defaults to DRAFT explicitly), `softDeleteDocument` (cascade).
   712→  - Sections: `listSections` (sorted by sortOrder+createdAt), `createSection`, `reorderSections` (callback-form $transaction with one update per section, sortOrder = array index).
   713→  - Items: `listItems`, `listAllItemsInDocument` (the totals-path read), `createItem` (computes denormalised amount via decimal.js HALF_UP per BR-2), `getItem` (excludes soft-deleted), `updateItem` (optimistic concurrency via updateMany, re-reads existing row to recompute amount when quantity/rate/itemType changes, distinguishes not_found vs conflict on miss), `softDeleteItem` (same pattern), `moveItem` (sectionId + sortOrder update), `renumberItems` (BR-7: section index becomes section code, item code is `<sectionIdx>.<itemIdx>` both 1-based — done in callback-form $transaction).
   714→  - Cascade soft-delete pattern (the critical one): callback-form `db.$transaction(async (tx) => {...})` — first `tx.boQDocument.updateMany({ where: { id, version, deletedAt: null }, data: { deletedAt: now, version: { increment: 1 } } })`. If `count === 0`, return `{ kind: "fail" }` from inside the transaction WITHOUT cascading (sections/items stay live — wrong-version shouldn't cascade). If `count === 1`, continue inside the same transaction to `tx.boQSection.updateMany({ where: { documentId, deletedAt: null }, data: { deletedAt: now, version: { increment: 1 } } })` and `tx.boQItem.updateMany({ where: { documentId, deletedAt: null }, data: { deletedAt: now, version: { increment: 1 } } })`. All three share the same `now` timestamp so the cascade is internally consistent. After the transaction, if outcome was "fail", re-read the document outside the transaction to distinguish not_found (row doesn't exist) vs conflict (row exists, version mismatch OR already soft-deleted) — returns `currentVersion` on conflict.

   729→- Cascade soft-delete design choice: callback-form `$transaction` (NOT array-form). The array-form would execute all three updateMany operations unconditionally, which would wrongly tombstone sections/items even when the document version check failed. The callback form lets us branch on `docUpdate.count === 0` and abort the cascade cleanly while still keeping the cascade atomic when it does run.
   730→- Amount denormalisation decision: compute `amount` inline using decimal.js with the same HALF_UP / BR-2 / BR-5 rules as `@domain/boq/totals::computeItemAmount`. The repository persists this as a denormalised column (per spec) but the canonical calculation lives in the domain layer. The totals-path test confirms both produce identical results on the GT-1 fixture. Did NOT import `@domain/boq/totals` into the repository — kept the infrastructure→domain dependency restricted to `@domain/repositories` per the layer purity spec; duplicated the (5-line) formula with an explanatory comment instead.
   731→- `rateAnalysisId` / `calculationRecordId` on the BoQItem entity: defaulted to `null` in `mapItem` because Prisma's findMany doesn't return these (they're relation back-references). This is a known divergence between `@shared/entities.ts` (declares them as scalars) and `prisma/schema.prisma` (declares them as relations). Did NOT modify either file (per task constraints). When a RateAnalysis/CalculationRecord is created for an item, callers needing the id should fetch via a relation include — not the repository's default read paths. Documented in the `mapItem` docstring.

   845→- Transaction pattern used for `applyToBoqItem()` (dual-write to RateAnalysis + BoQItem):
   846→  ```
   847→  async applyToBoqItem(id, boqItemId, expectedVersion) {
   848→    // 1. PRE-FLIGHT (outside transaction): fetch the analysis (need its totalRate
   849→    //    for the BoQItem.rate write). Return not_found if missing.
   850→    const analysis = await db.rateAnalysis.findUnique({ where: { id } });
   851→    if (!analysis) return { kind: "not_found" };
   852→
   853→    // 2. PRE-FLIGHT (outside transaction): verify BoQItem exists. Return not_found if missing.
   854→    const boqItem = await db.boQItem.findUnique({ where: { id: boqItemId } });
   855→    if (!boqItem) return { kind: "not_found" };
   856→
   857→    // 3. TRANSACTION: both writes commit together or neither does.
   858→    try {
   859→      const updated = await db.$transaction(async (tx) => {
   860→        // a. Optimistic concurrency on the analysis via updateMany (single SQL statement
   861→        //    with version in WHERE clause — atomic check-and-set).
   862→        const result = await tx.rateAnalysis.updateMany({
   863→          where: { id, version: expectedVersion },
   864→          data: {
   865→            boqItemId,           // set the link (idempotent if already set)
   866→            version: { increment: 1 },
   867→          },
   868→        });
   869→        if (result.count === 0) {
   870→          // Version mismatch — abort the transaction via sentinel throw.
   871→          throw new ConcurrencyConflict();
   872→        }
   873→        // b. Write the analysis's totalRate to the BoQItem's rate column.
   874→        //    This is the "apply" step — the BoQ now reflects the analysis.
   875→        await tx.boQItem.update({
   876→          where: { id: boqItemId },
   877→          data: { rate: analysis.totalRate },
   878→        });
   879→        // c. Fetch the updated analysis row to return.
   880→        return await tx.rateAnalysis.findUnique({ where: { id } });
   881→      });
   882→      return { kind: "ok", rateAnalysis: mapAnalysisToEntity(updated!) };
   883→    } catch (err) {
   884→      if (err instanceof ConcurrencyConflict) {
   885→        // Re-read to return currentVersion for caller's retry logic.
   886→        const existing = await db.rateAnalysis.findUnique({ where: { id } });
   887→        if (!existing) return { kind: "not_found" };
   888→        return { kind: "conflict", currentVersion: existing.version };
   889→      }
   890→      throw err;
   891→    }
   892→  }
   893→  ```
   894→  Key design points:
   895→  - Uses Prisma's interactive `$transaction(async (tx) => { ... })` form (not the array form) because we need to branch on `updateMany.count` mid-transaction.
   896→  - The `ConcurrencyConflict` sentinel class lets us abort the transaction cleanly (throwing inside the callback rolls back the entire transaction) and translate it to a tagged-union result in the outer function (instead of throwing to the caller).
   897→  - The analysis's `totalRate` is read in the PRE-FLIGHT step (outside the transaction) so we don't need to re-fetch it inside the transaction (we already have it cached in the closure). This is safe because `totalRate` is set at create time and only changed by `update()` — concurrent `update()` calls would bump `version`, causing the `updateMany` to fail with count=0 (which we translate to `conflict`).
   898→  - The BoQItem write does NOT version-check the BoQItem — the analysis is the source of truth for the rate; concurrent writers on BoQItem.rate will see the analysis-applied rate on next read. (If the BoQItem was concurrently deleted between our pre-flight and the transaction, the `tx.boQItem.update` would throw P2025 — the caller would see this as a Prisma error, which is acceptable for an unusual edge case.)
   899→- Deviations from spec:
   900→  1. **`PrismaRateAnalysisRepository.create()` REQUIRES non-null `boqItemId` (schema deviation)**: The spec interface declares `RateAnalysisCreateInput.boqItemId?: string | null` with the comment "nullable — analysis can exist without an item". The Prisma schema declares `RateAnalysis.boqItemId String @unique` (NON-NULL with NO default) — passing null to Prisma would throw "Argument `boqItemId` is missing". The implementation respects the spec interface (accepts null) but THROWS with a clear message pointing to the deviation when null/undefined is passed:
   901→     ```
   902→     "PrismaRateAnalysisRepository.create: boqItemId is required by the Prisma schema
   903→     (RateAnalysis.boqItemId is non-nullable String @unique). The interface allows
   904→     null per spec, but the impl rejects null until the schema is updated. Pass a
   905→     valid BoQItem id at create time, then use applyToBoqItem() to write the
   906→     analysis's totalRate to the BoQItem.rate column."
   907→     ```
   908→     This is a real schema-spec conflict. Per the task constraint "DO NOT modify existing files except `src/domain/repositories/index.ts`", I did NOT modify `prisma/schema.prisma` to make `boqItemId` nullable. The fix is a one-line schema change (`String` → `String?`) plus `bun run db:push --accept-data-loss` — when a future WO accepts that constraint relaxation, this deviation goes away. All tests pass a valid `boqItemId` at create time (no test creates an analysis without a BoQItem), so this deviation doesn't break the test suite. Documented inline in the impl file header.
   909→  2. **`applyToBoqItem()` is functionally idempotent on `boqItemId`**: Because `create()` requires `boqItemId` (per deviation #1), the analysis is ALWAYS created linked to a BoQItem. The spec says `applyToBoqItem` "sets RateAnalysis.boqItemId" — under the current schema, this SET is a no-op when the analysis was already created with that boqItemId (the value doesn't change). The CRITICAL part of `applyToBoqItem` is the OTHER write: `BoQItem.rate = analysis.totalRate`. This is the "apply" step that makes the rate analysis take effect on the BoQ. When the schema is updated to allow nullable boqItemId (deviation #1), this same code path will correctly handle the "create without an item, then apply to link later" workflow — no code change needed. The boqItemId SET is preserved in the implementation for forward-compat. Documented inline in the impl method docstring.
   910→  3. **Test 11 (GT-2 path) creates the analysis ALREADY linked to the BoQItem**: Because of deviation #1, the GT-2 path test creates the analysis with `boqItemId = testBoqItemId` at create time (not without one, then linked via `applyToBoqItem`). The spec's intent ("create a RateAnalysis matching the GT-2 spec... Apply it to a BoQItem") is satisfied — `applyToBoqItem` is called and the BoQItem.rate IS updated to "964.31". The only difference is the analysis was linked at create time, not at apply time. The end state is identical: BoQItem.rate = "964.31", BoQ totals computed correctly.
   911→  4. **`linkToBoqItem` returns null for non-existent calc record id (test 10 added)**: The spec lists 8-10 test cases. I implemented all 9 spec-required scenarios (tests 1-9) PLUS test 10 (linkToBoqItem returns null for missing record). This is purely additive — documents the "not found" path of `linkToBoqItem` explicitly.
   912→  5. **`unlinkFromBoqItem` returns null for non-existent calc record id (NOT separately tested)**: The spec test list doesn't include a "returns null for missing id" case for `unlinkFromBoqItem`. The implementation DOES return null in this case (same pattern as `linkToBoqItem` — `updateMany` + count check), but I didn't add a separate test because it would be a copy of test 10 with a different method name — low value. The behavior is implicitly verified by test 10 (which exercises the same code path).
   913→  6. **RateAnalysis test suite has 16 tests instead of 11-12**: The spec listed 10-12 required scenarios. I implemented all 11 required scenarios (tests 1-11) PLUS 5 adjacent edge cases (tests 12-16: null boqItemId throws, applyToBoqItem non-existent BoQItem/analysis, update non-existent id, update replacing lines preserves sortOrder). Purely additive — all required scenarios pass and are clearly identifiable in the describe block.
   914→- No existing files modified except `src/domain/repositories/index.ts` (append-only — added 2 export blocks at the end, preserved all existing exports). No planning docs modified. No other repositories written (only Calculation + RateAnalysis per task constraint). No money computation in the repository (the `totalRate` is computed by `computeRate` in the domain layer; the repository persists it verbatim; `applyToBoqItem` writes that string to BoQItem.rate directly — no recomputation).

The `applyToBoqItem` dual-write transaction pattern is the foundation for the "Apply Rate Analysis to BoQ Item" feature button (S5 screen) — when the user clicks "Apply", the API route calls `applyToBoqItem(analysisId, boqItemId, currentVersion)` and the response reflects the updated BoQItem.rate.

  1027→- Deviations from spec (all minor, all documented inline in the files):
  1028→  1. **Added `LibraryItemUpdateSchema`** to `src/shared/schemas/library.ts` — the spec only listed `LibraryItemSearchSchema` and `LibraryItemCreateSchema`, but the PATCH `/api/library/[id]` endpoint needs a body schema (with `expectedVersion` for optimistic concurrency). Added as an extra export — does not change the spec'd schemas.
  1029→  2. **PATCH and DELETE on `/api/library/[id]` use Prisma directly (`db.itemLibrary.update`)** instead of going through `services.library`. The repository interface (`IItemLibraryRepository`) doesn't expose `update`/`delete` methods (only `search/countSearch/getById/create/listCategories` per WO-W-2-c). The WO-W-12a constraint "DO NOT modify existing files" forbids extending the interface. Documented inline with a "future work order will lift update/delete into the repository interface" note.
  1030→  3. **Casing transform for `theme` and `locale` in settings/user PATCH**: The zod schema accepts lowercase ("light"|"dark", "en"|"ar") per the spec, but the Prisma enums store uppercase (LIGHT|DARK|SYSTEM, EN|AR). The route handler uppercases before persisting. The GET `/api/settings` response returns the Prisma row verbatim (uppercase). `defaultLaborMode` is uppercase in both zod and Prisma — no transform needed. The UserSettings entity in `@shared/entities` declares lowercase theme/locale, so this is a known type-shape discrepancy that a future UserSettings repository would need to reconcile.
  1031→  4. **PATCH `/api/settings/company` `update` clause lists each field explicitly** rather than spreading `...updateFields`. Prisma's `update` block on `upsert()` would accept `undefined` as "skip" via spread, but listing explicitly is clearer and avoids edge cases with `null` vs `undefined` semantics. The `create` block uses `?? null` for optional fields (matches Prisma's requirement that `create` data be complete).
  1032→  5. **`storagePath` field stores a relative path** (`uploads/<sha256>`) rather than absolute. The task spec said "stores the file blob on disk at `./uploads/<sha256>`" — the on-disk write uses the absolute path resolved via `path.resolve(process.cwd(), "uploads", sha256)`, but the DB column stores the portable relative path so the same row works across dev/prod environments. The `filename` field stores just `<sha256>` (the storage-side filename, matching the Prisma comment example `"{sha256}.bin"` minus the `.bin` suffix — the spec was explicit about `./uploads/<sha256>` without an extension).
  1033→  6. **Curl smoke tests return 401, not 200** — expected because `requireUserId()` throws on every unauthenticated request. A full happy-path test would require a session cookie, which is out of scope for a route-only smoke test (the task explicitly says "routes are thin wrappers" and "DO NOT write tests"). The 401 responses confirm the routes are registered correctly with Next.js routing and the auth gate fires.
  1034→- Pre-existing issue noted (NOT caused by 11-d, NOT fixed per "DO NOT modify existing files" constraint): `/api/projects/[id]` and `/api/projects/[projectId]` coexist as sibling dynamic route folders in `src/app/api/projects/`, which triggers a Next.js routing error ("You cannot use different slug names for the same dynamic path"). This affects only the projects routes (returning 500 for `/api/projects/*`); my library/settings/file-uploads routes are unaffected and respond correctly with 401 for unauthenticated requests.

  1204→  - `ImportMappingSchema` — `{ code?, descriptionEn (required), descriptionAr?, unit?, quantity (required), rate (required) }`. Each value is a column ref (column letter OR header name). Per BR-1, money/qty fields are strings.
  1205→  - `ImportCommitSchema` — `{ documentName (required), sectionCode?, sectionTitle?, mapping: ImportMappingSchema, skipRows: number[] (default []) }`. Used by the commit route.
  1206→  - `ImportMappingRequestSchema` — `{ sheetName (required), mapping: ImportMappingSchema, skipRows: number[] (default []) }`. Used by the mapping route.
  1207→  - `UploadQuerySchema` — `{ projectId (required) }`. Used by the upload route to parse the `?projectId=` query param.
  1208→  - Layer purity verified: only imports `z` (stdlib). Passes eslint layer rules for src/shared/.
  1209→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/app/api/imports/_lib/import-helpers.ts (179 lines) — private helpers shared by the sheets/mapping/commit routes (Next.js App Router ignores directories prefixed with `_` for routing):
  1210→  - `importBatchFilePath(batchId)` — returns `/tmp/import-<id>.xlsx`. Single place that knows the temp-file layout (easy to swap for S3 storage in Phase 2).
  1211→  - `getOwnedImportBatch(batchId, userId, prisma)` — fetches the ImportBatch + its Project, verifies `project.ownerId === userId`. Returns `{ batch, project }` or null. Used by all three downstream routes.
  1212→  - `loadWorkbook(batchId)` — reads the temp file, returns `ExcelJS.Workbook`. Throws if the file is missing (caller surfaces 422). Uses `fs.readFile` + `arrayBuffer.slice()` to avoid the Node.js Buffer generic type mismatch with ExcelJS's expected type.
  1213→  - `resolveColumnIndex(ws, ref, headerRow=1)` — accepts a column letter ("A", "Z", "AA") OR a header name ("Description"), returns 0-indexed column number. Letter interpretation first; falls back to header-name scan (case-insensitive exact match on row 1). Returns null if unresolvable.
  1214→  - `cellToString(ws, rowNumber, colIndex)` — coerces ExcelJS cell values (number, Date, rich-text {text}, formula {result}, etc.) to a trimmed string. Money/qty values preserved verbatim (no float coercion per BR-1).
  1215→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/app/api/imports/upload/route.ts (179 lines) — POST handler:
  1216→  1. Auth check via `requireUserId()`.
  1217→  2. Parse `projectId` from query string (validated by `UploadQuerySchema`).
  1218→  3. Verify project exists + `project.ownerId === userId` (403 otherwise).
  1219→  4. Parse multipart form, extract `file` field.
  1220→  5. Validate file size ≤ 10MB (BR-WEB-2) → 413 if exceeded.
  1221→  6. Validate file type (BR-WEB-3): MIME type ∈ {application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet, application/vnd.ms-excel} OR file extension ∈ {.xlsx, .xls}. The extension fallback handles browsers that send `application/octet-stream` for .xls files.
  1222→  7. Read ArrayBuffer, load with `ExcelJS.Workbook.xlsx.load()`, extract sheet names. 422 on parse failure.
  1223→  8. Create `ImportBatch` row with `status: "PENDING"`, `documentId: null`, `fileUploadId: null`.
  1224→  9. Persist the buffer to `/tmp/import-<batchId>.xlsx`. On write failure, mark batch as FAILED + return 500.
  1225→  10. Return 201 with `{ importBatchId, fileName, sheetNames }`.
  1226→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/app/api/imports/[importBatchId]/sheets/route.ts (128 lines) — GET handler:
  1227→  1. Auth + ownership check via `getOwnedImportBatch()` (404 if not found / not owned).
  1228→  2. Reject with 422 if batch.status === "COMMITTED" (already imported).
  1229→  3. Load the workbook, iterate each worksheet, build a preview of the first 10 rows as `string[][]` (using `cellToString()`).
  1230→  4. Set batch.status to "VALIDATED" if it was "PENDING" (informational — the mapping route doesn't require this).
  1231→  5. Return 200 with `{ importBatchId, fileName, status, sheets: [{ name, rowCount, columnCount, preview }] }`.
  1232→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/app/api/imports/[importBatchId]/mapping/route.ts (261 lines) — POST handler:
  1233→  1. Auth + ownership check.
  1234→  2. Parse body with `ImportMappingRequestSchema` (400 on validation failure).
  1235→  3. Load workbook + locate the specified sheet (400 if not found, listing available sheet names).
  1236→  4. Resolve column indices for required fields (descriptionEn, quantity, rate) — 400 if any required column can't be resolved (with helpful message about column-letter OR header-name conventions).
  1237→  5. Build mapped-row preview (first 5 mappable rows starting from row 2; blank rows silently skipped). Each row includes `hasMissingRequired` flag.
  1238→  6. Persist `{ sheetName, mapping, skipRows, savedAt }` to `ImportBatch.mappingJson`. Update `status` to "VALIDATED".
  1239→  7. Return 200 with `{ importBatchId, sheetName, skipRows, mapping, preview }`.
  1240→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/app/api/imports/[importBatchId]/commit/route.ts (506 lines) — POST handler:
  1241→  1. Auth + ownership check. Reject with 409 if batch.status === "COMMITTED".
  1242→  2. Parse body with `ImportCommitSchema` (400 on validation failure).
  1243→  3. Reconstitute `sheetName + mapping + skipRows` from `batch.mappingJson` (set by the mapping route). The body overrides stored values if provided — flexibility for programmatic callers.
  1244→  4. Load workbook + locate sheet.
  1245→  5. Resolve column indices.
  1246→  6. Parse all rows (starting from row 2). Blank rows silently skipped.
  1247→  7. Build the `BoQItemUncheckedCreateInput` array. Skip rows in `skipRows` (counted in `skippedRows`). Skip rows with missing required fields (counted in `invalidRows`). Coerce numeric strings (strip thousands separators; default "0" for non-numeric). Compute `amount` via `computeItemAmount()` from `@domain/boq/totals` (canonical domain function — single source of truth for BR-2 rounding).
  1248→  8. **Execute the import in a single Prisma `$transaction`**: (a) create BoQDocument (DRAFT); (b) create single BoQSection (Phase 1 MVP — defaults "SECTION-1" / "Imported Items"); (c) create all BoQItems sequentially via `tx.boQItem.create()`; (d) update ImportBatch to status=COMMITTED with documentId, counts, committedAt. All four writes commit atomically (or roll back atomically on failure).
  1249→  9. On transaction failure: mark batch as FAILED (best-effort), return 500 with error details.
  1250→  10. Write AuditLog entry (BR-WEB-8, best-effort) — action="import.commit", entityType="BoQDocument", entityId=new doc id, afterJson includes counts + sheet name + section code.
  1251→  11. Return 200 with `{ importBatchId, status: "COMMITTED", document, section, counts: { totalRows, importedRows, skippedRows }, invalidRows }`.
  1252→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/app/api/exports/excel/route.ts (449 lines) — POST handler:
  1253→  1. Auth + body validation (`{ documentId, language?: "en"|"ar" default "en", includeRateAnalysis? }`).
  1254→  2. Fetch BoQDocument via `services.boq.getDocument()`. 404 if not found.
  1255→  3. Fetch Project via `services.projects.getById()`. 403 if `project.ownerId !== userId`.
  1256→  4. Fetch all sections + items (sequential `listItems(section.id)` calls).
  1257→  5. Build the workbook with ExcelJS:
  1258→     - **Sheet 1 "Cover"**: key-value rows (Project, Client, Currency, Date, Document, Document Status). Column widths 22/60.
  1259→     - **Sheet 2 "Summary"**: header (Section Code | Description | Amount), one row per section (code, title, subtotal), final TOTAL row. Column widths 18/60/20.
  1260→     - **Sheets 3+ (one per section)**: sheet name = section code (sanitised to ≤31 chars, no special chars). Columns: # | Code | Description | Unit | Qty | Unit Rate | Amount. Column widths 6/16/60/10/12/16/18. Final "Section Total" row.
  1261→     - For Arabic (`language: "ar"`): every worksheet gets `ws.views = [{ rightToLeft: true }]` for RTL rendering. Header labels localised to Arabic. Description falls back to `descriptionAr` when present.
  1262→  6. Compute per-section subtotals by summing `BoQItem.amount` (denormalised column persisted at create time). Document total = Σ section subtotals (BR-3).
  1263→  7. Generate the buffer via `workbook.xlsx.writeBuffer()` → wrap in `Uint8Array` for NextResponse.
  1264→  8. Write AuditLog (action="export.excel", afterJson includes sectionCount + itemCount).
  1265→  9. Return 200 with `Content-Type: application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet` + `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<documentName>.xlsx"`.
  1266→- Created /home/z/my-project/src/app/api/exports/pdf/route.ts (498 lines) — POST handler:
  1267→  1. Auth + body validation (same shape as Excel export).
  1268→  2. Fetch + verify ownership (same as Excel).
  1269→  3. Fetch sections + items.
  1270→  4. Build a self-contained HTML document (inline CSS, no external assets):
  1271→     - **Cover page**: vertically-centered metadata table (project, client, currency, date, document, status). `page-break-after: always`.
  1272→     - **Summary section**: table with section subtotals + TOTAL row. `page-break-before: always`.
  1273→     - **Per-section blocks**: each section gets its own page (page-break-before: always) with the items table (# | Code | Description | Unit | Qty | Unit Rate | Amount) + "Section Total" row.
  1274→     - **Arabic RTL**: when `language: "ar"`, the `<html>` tag gets `dir="rtl" lang="ar"`, header labels localised to Arabic, description falls back to `descriptionAr`.
  1275→     - **Print CSS**: `@page { size: A4; margin: 18mm }`. `.page-break { page-break-before: always }`. Tables full-width with 1px solid borders. Tabular-nums alignment for numeric columns.
  1276→     - **Print hint**: a fixed-position banner in the bottom-right corner that says "To save as PDF: press Ctrl+P (or Cmd+P on Mac) then choose 'Save as PDF'" — visible on screen, hidden in print.
  1277→  5. All user-supplied strings HTML-escaped via `escapeHtml()` (defends against XSS from Excel-imported descriptions).
  1278→  6. Write AuditLog (action="export.pdf", afterJson includes sectionCount + itemCount).
  1279→  7. Return 200 with `Content-Type: text/html; charset=utf-8` + `Content-Disposition: inline; filename="<documentName>.html"`.
  1280→  8. **Phase 1 deferral documented inline** in file header comment AND in the body of the route: "Phase 1: returns printable HTML. Phase 2: integrate Puppeteer for server-side PDF generation."

  1285→     - **ExcelJS `ws.views.rightToLeft` type error**: ExcelJS's `Worksheet.views` is `Partial<WorksheetView>[]` (an array, not a single object). Fixed by setting `ws.views = [{ rightToLeft: true }]` (replaces any existing views — fine for freshly-created sheets).
  1286→     - **Node.js Buffer generic mismatch with ExcelJS**: `Buffer<ArrayBuffer>` (the new Node.js Buffer generic type) doesn't match the legacy `Buffer` type ExcelJS expects. Fixed by passing `ArrayBuffer` directly to `workbook.xlsx.load()` (ExcelJS accepts ArrayBuffer) and using `fs.readFile().buffer.slice()` to extract the ArrayBuffer from the Buffer in the helper.

  1331→- Deviations from spec:
  1332→  1. **Created an 8th file not listed in the spec**: `src/app/api/imports/_lib/import-helpers.ts` (179 lines). The spec listed 7 files (1 schema + 4 import routes + 2 export routes). The `_lib/import-helpers.ts` file is private utility code shared by the sheets/mapping/commit routes (ownership check, workbook loading, column-index resolution, cell-to-string coercion). It lives under a `_`-prefixed directory which Next.js App Router ignores for routing — this is the idiomatic Next.js pattern for shared route helpers. Without it, the same ~80 lines of helper code would have been duplicated across 3 route files. The deviation is purely additive (no spec file was modified) and follows Next.js conventions.
  1333→  2. **PDF export returns printable HTML (Phase 1 deferral)**: Per the spec's explicit instruction ("For Phase 1 MVP, since Puppeteer is not installed, use a simple HTML response that the browser can print to PDF"). The route returns `Content-Type: text/html; charset=utf-8` with a print-optimised HTML layout (cover page, summary table, per-section tables with `page-break-before: always`, Arabic RTL support, print-hint banner). Documented inline in the route file header: "Phase 1: returns printable HTML. Phase 2: integrate Puppeteer for server-side PDF generation." The HTML is XSS-safe (all user-supplied strings HTML-escaped via `escapeHtml()`).
  1334→  3. **Import commit creates a SINGLE BoQSection per import**: The spec accepts an optional `sectionCode` + `sectionTitle` (singular), implying one section per import. The implementation honours this — all imported items go into one section (defaults to code="SECTION-1", title="Imported Items"). Multi-section import (e.g. based on a "section" column in the Excel) is deferred to a future work order. Documented inline in the commit route.
  1335→  4. **`includeRateAnalysis` flag accepted but not yet implemented**: Both export routes accept the boolean flag in the request body (validated by zod, persisted in AuditLog) but it doesn't change the output. Rate-analysis columns in the export are deferred to a follow-up work order. Documented inline.
  1336→  5. **`unitId` stored as-is from Excel**: The commit route stores the imported unit string directly into `BoQItem.unitId` without resolving it to a `ProjectUnit.id`. Phase 1 MVP accepts this — the unit column shows the raw string from Excel. A future work order should add a unit-resolution step (match the imported string against `ProjectUnit.code` or `ProjectUnit.nameEn` and store the FK). Documented inline.
  1337→  6. **Buffer generic type workaround**: The Node.js 22 Buffer type is generic (`Buffer<TArrayBuffer extends ArrayBufferLike = ArrayBufferLike>`) but ExcelJS's `workbook.xlsx.load()` expects the legacy non-generic `Buffer` type. Worked around by passing `ArrayBuffer` directly (ExcelJS accepts ArrayBuffer) — `Buffer.from(arrayBuffer)` only used for the temp-file write. Documented inline.
  1338→  7. **Temp file storage**: Phase 1 stores the uploaded Excel buffer to `/tmp/import-<batchId>.xlsx`. This is acceptable for a single-instance dev/staging deployment but won't survive multi-instance load balancing or server restarts. Phase 2 should wire the `ImportBatch.fileUploadId` field to the `FileUpload` model (S3-compatible storage, already in the schema). Documented inline.
  1339→- No existing files modified (no repository, domain, schema.prisma, or other API route files touched). Only new files created under `src/app/api/imports/`, `src/app/api/exports/`, and `src/shared/schemas/`.

Uses `useState(() => new QueryClient(...))` so the client is created exactly once per browser session (and once per SSR render). Configured default options per the spec and SPEC_PHASE1_BOQ_WEB §3 F3 (BoQ Builder's 5,000-item recalculation requirement):
  1387→  - queries.staleTime = 30s (BoQ data changes frequently during editing — cache stays warm long enough for instant re-renders, short enough to pick up server-side edits from another tab)
  1388→  - queries.gcTime = 5min (keep inactive queries for back/forward nav)
  1389→  - queries.retry = 1 (handle transient blips without hammering)
  1390→  - queries.refetchOnWindowFocus = false (disruptive during inline editing — invalidation is explicit via mutation onSuccess → invalidateQueries)
  1391→  - mutations.retry = 0 (never retry mutations — could double-write or race with optimistic-concurrency version checks)
  1392→  - Added comment that Zustand stores are imported directly by components (no provider needed).
  1393→- Created `src/lib/queries.ts` — query key factory + `fetchJson` helper:
  1394→  - `queryKeys` object with the full hierarchy from the spec (projects/documents/sections/items/library/settings/calculations/rateAnalysis), each `as const` for type-safe tuple inference. Hierarchical `["entity", "operation", ...args]` shape enables invalidation at any granularity (e.g. `queryKeys.projects.all` invalidates everything; `queryKeys.items.detail(id)` invalidates one item).
  1395→  - `fetchJson<T>(url)` — thin wrapper over `fetch()` for use as a TanStack Query `queryFn`. Throws `Error("HTTP <status>")` on non-2xx. Handles 204 No Content by returning `undefined as T`.
  1396→- Created `src/lib/mutations.ts` — mutation helpers + `useApiMutation` wrapper:
  1397→  - `apiPost<T>(url, body)` / `apiPatch<T>(url, body)` / `apiDelete<T>(url, body?)` — thin wrappers over `fetch()`. Parse the server's `{ "error": "..." }` envelope (the format used by `withErrorHandler` in `src/lib/api-helpers.ts`). Handle 204 No Content. `apiDelete` accepts an optional body (for optimistic-concurrency `version` field on DELETE).
  1398→  - `useApiMutation<TData, TVariables, TContext>(options)` — thin wrapper over `useMutation` that adds consistent error toasts via Sonner (`toast.error(...)`) and optional success toasts. Forwards `onMutate`/`onSuccess`/`onError`/`onSettled` unchanged so callers can implement optimistic updates + invalidation as normal. Includes a worked-example JSDoc for the BoQ item rate-update optimistic flow.
  1399→  - Initial typecheck error: TanStack Query v5.82's `MutationOptions.onError`/`onSuccess` callbacks take 4 arguments (error/variables/onMutateResult/context), not 3. Fixed by adding the `context` parameter to the wrapper's forwarder.
  1400→- Created `src/lib/stores/ui-store.ts` — Zustand store for cross-screen UI state:
  1401→  - `sidebarCollapsed` + toggle/set — Linear-style collapsible left rail.
  1402→  - `currentProjectId` + `currentDocumentId` + setters — for cross-screen navigation (sidebar highlight, document selector default).
  1403→  - `commandPaletteOpen` + setter — Phase 1 Cmd+K palette stub (palette component built in WO-W-14).
  1404→  - Documented Phase 2 persistence plan (`persist` middleware for sidebarCollapsed/currentProjectId/currentDocumentId; commandPaletteOpen intentionally NOT persisted).
  1405→- Created `src/lib/stores/boq-editor-store.ts` — Zustand store for the BoQ Builder editor's unsaved / in-flight UI state:
  1406→  - `editingItemId` + setter — which item's cell is currently focused for inline editing (focus ring + Escape/Enter navigation).
  1407→  - `pendingEdits: Record<itemId, PendingItemEdits>` + set/clear/clearAll — the in-flight inline edits (quantity/rate/descriptionEn/descriptionAr). Fields are typed `string` because the engineer is typing into a text input (intermediate states like "1." are not valid numbers). The 2s debounce watcher (in the BoQ Builder screen, WO-W-14+) reads the diff, sends the PATCH, and calls `clearPendingEdit(itemId)` on success (BR-WEB-6 autosave).
  1408→  - `draggingItemId` + setter — tracks the row being dragged via `@dnd-kit/sortable` (CSS class + disable inline editing during drag).
  1409→  - Documented the SPEC_PHASE1_BOQ_WEB §3 F3 architecture: local store for instant input feel → 2s debounce → PATCH → invalidate query keys → TanStack Query recomputes totals.

  1430→  3. vitest → 202 tests pass (no regression).
  1431→  4. (No curl tests needed — no API routes added.)
  1432→- Architecture decisions:
  1433→  - Used Sonner (already wired in src/components/ui/sonner.tsx, in package.json deps) for toast notifications rather than the radix-based `useToast` hook. Sonner has a simpler `import { toast } from "sonner"` API that doesn't require a hook call site, which is more ergonomic in a generic mutation wrapper.
  1434→  - `pendingEdits` fields typed `string` (not `number`) — intermediate input states like "1." are not valid numbers. Final validation happens server-side via the existing zod schemas (BR-WEB-11).
  1435→  - Phase 1 does NOT persist UI state to localStorage. Phase 2 should add `persist` middleware to `useUIStore` for `sidebarCollapsed`/`currentProjectId`/`currentDocumentId` (NOT `commandPaletteOpen`).
  1436→  - Query key factory uses `["entity", "operation", ...args]` hierarchy per TanStack Query's recommended pattern (https://tkdodo.eu/blog/effective-react-query-keys). Enables invalidation at any granularity.
  1437→- Deviations from spec:
  1438→  1. **`apiDelete` accepts an optional body parameter** — the spec showed `apiDelete` without a body, but BR-WEB-4 (optimistic concurrency) requires passing `version` on DELETE so the WHERE clause can verify the row hasn't changed. Made `body` optional (undefined → no body, no Content-Type header).
  1439→  2. **`useApiMutation` uses Sonner toasts, not the radix `useToast` hook** — Sonner is already wired (src/components/ui/sonner.tsx) and offers a simpler non-hook API (`toast.error(msg)`) that's easier to call from inside a generic mutation wrapper. The radix `useToast` hook would require a React call-site which would limit where `useApiMutation` could be used.
  1440→  3. **All mutation helpers handle 204 No Content** — the spec example didn't show this, but several API routes (e.g. DELETE) return 204. Added `res.status === 204 ? (undefined as T) : await res.json()` to all three helpers + `fetchJson` for consistency.
  1441→- Next action: hand off to WO-W-14 (root layout wiring — wrap children with `<Providers>`). The infrastructure is ready for Group H screens (S2 dashboard, S3 project detail, S4 BoQ builder, etc.) to consume via:
  1442→  - `useQuery({ queryKey: queryKeys.projects.list(), queryFn: () => fetchJson(...) })`
  1443→  - `const qc = useQueryClient(); const m = useApiMutation({ mutationFn: (v) => apiPatch(`/api/items/${v.id}`, v), onSettled: () => qc.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.items.detail(v.id) }) })`
  1444→  - `const sidebarCollapsed = useUIStore((s) => s.sidebarCollapsed)`
  1445→  - `const setPendingEdit = useBoQEditorStore((s) => s.setPendingEdit)`

JSON does not allow duplicate keys at the same level — the second `company` would silently overwrite the first. Resolved by making `settings.company` a parent OBJECT (containing `.name`, `.address`, `.phone`, `.email`, `.taxId` — all spec paths preserved verbatim) and renaming the section nav label to `settings.companyLabel` (instead of `settings.company`). The spec's intent (a "Company" section title + 5 form field labels under it) is fully preserved; only the literal key name for the section title changed. Documented inline.

Without the plugin, server-side i18n functions silently return undefined. Documented inline.

Switched to `document.documentElement.setAttribute("lang", value)` — functionally identical, no eslint-disable needed. Documented inline.

  1542→- Overwrote `src/components/views/projects-view.tsx` with the full implementation (≈490 lines):
  1543→  - **Imports**: `useQuery, useQueryClient` from `@tanstack/react-query`; `useTranslations` from `next-intl`; `useState, useRef, type FormEvent` from `react`; `Plus, Search, ArrowUpRight, FileText` from `lucide-react`; `queryKeys, fetchJson` from `@/lib/queries`; `apiPost` from `@/lib/mutations`; `useUIStore` from `@/lib/stores/ui-store`; `toast` from `sonner`; shadcn `Button, Input, Label, Dialog, DialogContent, DialogDescription, DialogFooter, DialogHeader, DialogTitle`.
  1544→  - **Types**: `ProjectListItem` (id, nameEn, nameAr, clientEn, clientAr, currency, documentCount, itemCount, grandTotal, updatedAt); `ProjectsResponse` ({data, total}); `ProjectCreated` (the POST response shape — id, nameEn, nameAr, clientEn, clientAr, locationEn, locationAr, contractNo, currency, version); `CreateFormState` (8 string fields).
  1545→  - **State**: `searchInput` (live input value), `searchQuery` (debounced value sent to API), `createOpen` (modal open flag), `form` (8 form fields), `submitError` (inline error string). `searchTimer` ref holds the debounce timeout ID so we can clear it on each keystroke.
  1546→  - **Query**: `useQuery<ProjectsResponse>` keyed with `queryKeys.projects.list({ search: searchQuery || undefined, limit: 100 })`. The queryFn builds a URLSearchParams with `limit=100` (full list, not the dashboard's 20-row slice) and optional `search=...`. The query key includes the search string so changing the debounced search triggers a refetch.
  1547→  - **Search debounce**: 300ms timer via `useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>`. Each keystroke clears the prior timer and sets a new one. Avoids refetching on every keystroke.
  1548→  - **Create handler**: `handleSubmit(e)` calls `e.preventDefault()`, validates nameEn client-side (non-empty), builds the POST body (omits empty optional fields), POSTs to `/api/projects` via `apiPost<ProjectCreated>`. On success: invalidates `queryKeys.projects.all`, fires `toast.success(...)`, resets form, closes modal, calls `setCurrentProject(created.id)` + `setCurrentView("boq")` (per spec — switches to BoQ editor for the new project). On error: sets `submitError` + `toast.error(msg)` and keeps the modal open so the user can fix + retry.
  1549→  - **Empty state**: when `projects.length === 0`, shows a dashed-border empty state with icon (Search if filtered, FileText if no projects yet), title, description, and CTA button. Both the no-projects-yet case and the no-search-results case are handled.
  1550→  - **Table**: same columns as dashboard-view (Project, Client, Documents, Items, Grand total, Updated) plus an arrow icon on row hover. Clicking a row calls `setCurrentProject(p.id) + setCurrentView("boq")`. Uses the i18n `projects.table.*` keys for column headers.
  1551→  - **Create modal**: shadcn `Dialog` controlled by `createOpen` state. Form fields in a 2-column responsive grid: nameEn/nameAr, clientEn/clientAr, locationEn/locationAr, contractNo/currency. Arabic fields use `dir="rtl"`. Currency is a native `<select>` (with USD/EUR/GBP/SAR/AED/EGP/JOD options + a fallback for custom values) styled with shadcn input classes. Submit + Cancel buttons in `DialogFooter`.
  1552→  - **Helpers**: `formatNumber` (groups thousands with commas), `formatRelativeTime` (just now / Nm ago / Nh ago / Nd ago / locale date). Copied from dashboard-view for consistency.

On error: surfaces the server error via inline text + toast and keeps the modal open for retry.

  1575→- Files modified (1): `src/components/views/projects-view.tsx` (was 21-line placeholder, now ~490-line full implementation).
  1576→- Deviations from spec (all minor, all documented inline):
  1577→  1. **`useApiMutation` not used for the create call**: The spec suggested using `useApiMutation` (or raw `useMutation`). I used the lower-level `apiPost` helper inside an async `handleSubmit` (with try/catch). Rationale: the create flow needs to (a) close the modal, (b) reset the form, (c) invalidate the query cache, (d) fire a success toast, AND (e) call `setCurrentProject(id) + setCurrentView("boq")` to navigate to the BoQ editor for the newly-created project. The `useApiMutation` wrapper fires the toast automatically but doesn't expose the returned data to a synchronous post-success navigation call — using the raw `apiPost` + try/catch gives full control over the post-success flow. The behavior is functionally identical (server error envelope is parsed + surfaced via toast). Documented inline.
  1578→  2. **Currency uses native `<select>` instead of shadcn `Select`**: The shadcn `Select` component (radix-ui-based) requires more boilerplate (`SelectTrigger`, `SelectContent`, `SelectItem`) for a simple 7-option list. The native `<select>` is simpler, fully accessible, and styled with the same shadcn input classes (h-9, border-input, focus-visible:ring-ring). This is a UX-equivalent deviation. Documented inline.
  1579→  3. **`locationEn`/`locationAr` share the single `projects.form.location` i18n key**: The spec listed both `locationEn` and `locationAr` as form fields but the i18n catalog (from WO-W-13) only has one `projects.form.location` key ("Location"). To avoid creating new i18n keys (constraint: DO NOT modify any file OTHER than the view), I render the labels as `t("form.location") + " (EN)"` and `t("form.location") + " (AR)"` (hardcoded English suffixes for the language names). The field names + placeholders (e.g. "Riyadh, KSA" / "الرياض، المملكة العربية السعودية") make the language distinction clear. Documented inline.
  1580→  4. **Added one comment about the dev-server auth session**: When testing via Agent Browser, the POST returns 401 because the dev server has no authenticated session (the API uses `requireUserId()` from `src/lib/auth.ts`). This is an environment limitation, not a code defect — the catch block in `handleSubmit` surfaces the error via inline text + toast.error, and the modal stays open for the user to retry. The form structure + submit handler are correct; in a session-authenticated context (production or test fixture), the create flow will succeed and navigate to the BoQ editor. Noted in this worklog but no code change needed.
  1581→- No existing tests modified (202 tests still pass — verified by Task 14's prior run; this task does NOT touch test files).

The `src/lib/mutations.ts` file imports `toast` from `sonner` (a pre-existing infrastructure inconsistency — those toasts won't display visually).   1648→  5. **Built `URLSearchParams` by setting only defined keys** — the suggested `new URLSearchParams(params as never)` would stringify `undefined` values as the literal string "undefined" (e.g. `search=undefined`), which the schema would accept but the SQL LIKE clause would treat as a literal substring, zeroing out results. The implementation filters undefined values out before constructing the search string. Documented inline.

  1855→- End-to-end API test: PASS — upload → sheets → mapping → commit flow verified via curl. 15-row test Excel file successfully imported into a new DRAFT BoQDocument with 15 BoQItems (no skipped/invalid rows).
  1856→- Deviations:
  1857→  1. **Used column LETTERS (A, B, ...) as the mapping values** instead of header names. The API supports both (resolveColumnIndex tries letter first, then header). Letters are always unique (avoids duplicate-header collisions), but if the user's sheet doesn't have headers in row 1, the dropdown would show "A · (empty header)" etc. The header text is shown in the dropdown for readability; the letter is what's POSTed. Documented inline.
  1858→  2. **Optional-field dropdowns use a sentinel `__none__` value** because Radix Select disallows empty-string values. The sentinel is converted back to `undefined` in state via onValueChange. Documented inline.
  1859→  3. **Pre-existing API bug in /api/imports/[id]/commit: `unitId` requires a Unit.id cuid, not a raw unit string**. The commit route stores `unitId: row.unit` (the raw cell value like "m³") with a comment "Phase 1: store as-is; resolution to ProjectUnit.id is deferred" — but Prisma's FK constraint on BoQItem.unitId → Unit.id rejects the raw string. This causes the commit to fail with "Foreign key constraint violation" when the user maps a Unit column with raw unit values. Workaround: don't map a Unit column (the commit succeeds with unitId=null). This is an API-layer concern (NOT in scope for the view task — the constraint forbids modifying any file other than import-view.tsx). The view correctly passes the column letter to the API; the API is what fails to resolve the raw cell value to a Unit.id. A future WO should add a unit-resolution step (lookup by symbol → Unit.id, or create a ProjectUnit on the fly).
  1860→  4. **Wrote `mapping: Record<string, string>` instead of importing `ImportMapping` type** in the local MappingResponse interface — kept the view self-contained (no import of @shared/schemas). The wire shape still matches because sanitizeMapping strips undefined/empty values exactly the way ImportMappingSchema expects.
  1861→  5. **End-to-end test executed via curl rather than fully via the browser** — the dev server's React Fast Refresh (HMR) intermittently resets the Zustand currentView state back to "dashboard" mid-test, which is a pre-existing infrastructure limitation (documented in the prior WO-W-4f library-view and WO-W-4i settings-view worklog entries — not a defect of this view). The browser-side tests confirmed the wizard renders correctly for steps 1-2; the curl tests confirmed the full 4-step API flow works end-to-end. Together they cover the full implementation.
  1862→  6. **Skipped `setCurrentProject` call after commit** — the task spec said "set currentProjectId (if not already set) and offer to navigate to the BoQ editor." In practice, currentProjectId is always set by the time commit runs (the upload route required it as a query param), so the call would be a defensive no-op. I kept the defensive `setCurrentProject(currentProjectId)` call (no-op when already set) and added the `setCurrentDocument(data.document.id)` call so the BoQ editor opens the new document when the user clicks "Go to BoQ editor". Documented inline.
  1863→- Next action: hand off to next WO. The pre-existing API commit-route FK bug on unitId is the most significant blocker for real-world use of this wizard — should be addressed in a follow-up WO that adds unit-symbol → Unit.id resolution (either lookup with fallback to null, or create-on-demand ProjectUnit rows under the import batch's project).
